About Fino · Corrections

Corrections

Fino gets some things wrong. When one is found, the page is fixed and the fix is listed here, newest first.

Corrections
418
First
5 November 2026
Latest
18 November 2026

Fino reads decisions from regulators in many countries and languages, and gets some details wrong: a date, an amount, the name of an organisation, the state of an appeal. Each entry says what the page said and what it says now, with a link to the page and, where the right fact is written elsewhere, a link to that source.

The list shows only the errors Fino found or was told about. A page that is not on it is not proven correct.

Found an error? Write to hello@finoprivacy.com and quote the decision number. If you can, say where the right fact is written.

When a correction concerns a private person, a sole trader or a small partnership, the detail is withheld: Fino does not name them. This page leaves out the detail of 24 entries; the data file holds the full list. Not legal advice.

C-0417Decision 2024/SI/001

What was wrong
The Fino list had one decision twice: 2024/SI/002 and 2024/SI/001, the same decision (same regulator reference and document).
What it says now
2024/SI/002 is no longer a page of its own: its address forwards to 2024/SI/001.

C-0412The fines page

What was wrong
The list of GDPR articles on 8 Slovenia pages came from the CMS Enforcement Tracker or GDPRhub and differed from the articles the decision itself cites. The topic labels drawn from the articles change with them.
What it says now
Each page now lists the articles the decision finds infringed or applies, read in the original; where the decision cites none, no article is listed. The topic labels follow the articles. 2021/SI/006: none listed (was Art. 6); 2023/SI/005: Art. 6(1), Art. 17(1) (was Art. 17(1), Art. 17(3)(b), Art. 58(2)); 2024/SI/001: Art. 12(3), Art. 15(1), Art. 6(1) (was Art. 12, Art. 12(3), Art. 15, Art. 15(1)); 2024/SI/006: none listed (was Art. 17, Art. 85(1)); 2025/SI/002: Art. 12(1), Art. 13 (was Art. 12, Art. 13); 2025/SI/010: Art. 5(1)(a), Art. 6(1), Art. 9(2) (was Art. 6(1), Art. 9); 2026/SI/002: Art. 25(1) (was Art. 25); 2026/SI/003: Art. 12(1), Art. 13, Art. 32 (was Art. 32).

One page, 2022/RO/028, shows RON 34,600.30 with both fines (RON 9,885.80 and RON 24,714.50). The old addresses of 2022/RO/052 and 2022/RO/053 forward to it, and the two extra EUR 7,000 are out of the totals.

Joined after reading the sources: 2024/PL/006 and 2024/PL/031 (UODO DKN.5130.2415.2020, 12 November 2024), 2026/PL/020 and 2026/PL/024, 2023/ES/077 and 2023/ES/273 (AEPD PS-00218-2023), 2019/BE/002 and ND/BE/001 (decision 13/2019), 2023/BG/002 and 2023/BG/008, 2024/GR/020 (the total of EUR 400,000 and EUR 40,000) with 2024/GR/002, 2021/GR/021 (the total of two fines of EUR 20,000) with 2021/GR/009, 2021/IS/031 (ISK 11,500,000, the ministry's ISK 7,500,000 and YAY's ISK 4,000,000) with 2021/IS/002, and 2020/HU/017 with 2020/HU/016. 2020/HU/016 shows HUF 100,000 (the decision), not GDPRhub's EUR 13,136, and 2021/IS/002 shows ISK 7,500,000. Same-day, same-amount pairs of other countries are listed for a later check.

C-0136The fines page

What was wrong
The decisions portal of the Polish regulator (UODO) records court steps in the history of each decision, but its histories sometimes hang a later step on the wrong decision, and the portal often lags behind the courts; meanwhile 30 decisions showed no court outcome, and 2020/PL/012 and 2022/PL/010 said only that the portal heads them "uchylona" (set aside) without saying who set them aside.
What it says now
32 decisions now carry a court status taken from the court database (CBOSA, orzeczenia.nsa.gov.pl), each with the court, the case number and the judgment's own words, and checked against the decision's date in the judgment and on the portal: confirmed in court 18 (16 final, 2 not final), annulled 10 (7 final, 3 not final: 2023/PL/002, 2023/PL/007, 2025/PL/016), under court challenge 3 (2021/PL/005, 2022/PL/012, 2022/PL/013), final 1 (2025/PL/015). Annulled fines count nowhere; the annulled decisions include 2019/PL/004, 2020/PL/003 and 2021/PL/009 (the fines of ID Finance Poland and Cyfrowy Polsat), 2020/PL/005 and 2020/PL/012, 2022/PL/010 and 2023/PL/003. 2020/PL/012 and 2022/PL/010 now name the Supreme Administrative Court. Left out: 2020/PL/011 and 2024/PL/019 (a complaint rejected as inadmissible: the decision stands), 2021/PL/006 (the Supreme Administrative Court sent the case back), 2021/PL/010 and 2022/PL/011 (the judgments cite a different month from the portal's date), 2024/PL/013, 015 and 018 (a "Rozpatrzenie" entry that does not say what it was) and 30 decisions the portal marks as not final with no court step.

C-0086Decision 2021/DE/051

What was wrong

What it says now

What it says now
The Garante's decision text gives 13 April 2023 (docweb 9897055).

C-0002Decision 2025/AT/037

What was wrong
The page, then numbered ND/AT/001, gave the decision date as 28 October 2026. The date came from a typing error at the source.
What it says now
The Austrian Data Protection Authority decided on 28 October 2025 (file 2025-0.861.933), and the page is now numbered 2025/AT/037. The old address ND/AT/001 forwards to the new one.

Found by a Fino checkWhere the right fact comes from ↗

C-0001Decision 2026/IT/172

What was wrong
The page, then numbered 2025/IT/081, showed 16 January 2025 as the decision date (the right day in the wrong year) and the summary and the GDPRhub link of another Garante decision (docweb 10112709), because the two had been merged by mistake as one decision.
What it says now
The page is its own decision: a hospital authority in Benevento, decided 16 January 2026 (docweb 10218564), now numbered 2026/IT/172, with its own summary and source. The other decision has its own page, 2025/IT/092. The old address 2025/IT/081 forwards to the new one.

Found by a Fino checkWhere the right fact comes from ↗
